# Tests allowed to exceed the default lane's time budget.
#
# Read by scripts/report_test_durations.py in CI (.github/workflows/ci.yml).
# One pytest node id per line, then " # " and the reason. The budget, from
# tests/README.md (MADDENING's):
#
#   up to 1 s   fine
#   1-5 s       watch list; optimise when you are in the file
#   over 5 s    mark @pytest.mark.slow (which runs in no CI job yet), make
#               it much faster, or keep it here with
#               "kept: <why it must run on every push>"
#
# An unlisted test over 5 s gets a warning on the run. Since 2026-11-09 (CONTRIBUTING.md,
# "CI: the duration gate"): over 30 s, once re-run alone, it fails the job if it is the
# change's own (its file is in the diff, or its node id is new on the base), and any other
# unlisted test fails only over 90 s. A listed test over 30 s passes with a warning, and
# over its ceiling (3 times its weekly median, at least 60 s, tests/duration_ceilings.json,
# written by scripts/duration_triage.py, never by hand) it fails a push and warns a pull
# request unless the pull request owns the test. The entries below that quote 20 s and
# 17 s were listed under the earlier line of 20 s and stay: they are measurements.
#
# A property test is never shrunk and never marked slow to meet the budget:
# slow runs in no CI job, so a slow-marked property is checked nowhere.

# Added from the CI runs of 2026-11-07 (65 runs, 2,797 shard reports): the tests not listed above that were over 20 s at least once, the
# standing rule in CONTRIBUTING.md ('CI: borderline tests') applied over every run read. No test was in the second half of that rule
# (over 17 s in a quarter of the runs) without being in the first. Each entry says why the test stays on every push, then its largest
# cold time and runner, its median, a restored-cache median where a shard restored one, and how often it was over 20 s.
